People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, Doctor. Thank you for fitting me in, I really appreciate it. 

Um... well, I guess what brought me in is that I just... I don't really feel like myself anymore? I hope it's okay to describe it that way. It's probably nothing too serious, but I've noticed I barely talk to people lately. I just sort of go... flat and quiet, I guess. I'm a building cleaner, and usually I'm fine with the work, but recently, even just getting started on my cleaning route feels like pushing a loaded cart uphill. 

I mean, I'm still getting my job done, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just being lazy or anything! But... does that make sense? Is it normal to just feel that blank and heavy?

Um... I know we just touched on this a little bit, and I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much or taking up too much of your time. 

But, yeah, I guess I have lost interest. Like I was saying, I used to really love my Saturday fishing trips, and just having my coffee with the crossword puzzle in the mornings. Um... sad or empty? Well, I wouldn't want to use a strong word like "empty." That sounds so dramatic, doesn't it? I really don't want to come across like I'm whining or just feeling sorry for myself. 

I guess... maybe I feel a little down sometimes. Just kind of flat, you know? Like I'm just going through the motions. But I wouldn't say I'm sitting around crying or anything like that. I still get up and go to work and do my job. I try to keep a smile on my face so the people in the building don't have to deal with my bad moods. 

Is it okay to just feel a little flat sometimes? I hope that's a normal way to feel at my age. I really do try to stay positive as much as I can.

Um... weight changes? Well, I don't want you to think I'm not taking care of myself or anything. I'm really trying to keep up with things.

Um... moving more slowly? Well, I don't want you to think I'm slacking off at work or anything. I always make sure my section of the building is completely done before I clock out, I really do.

Um... guilty? Oh, I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, really I do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around complaining.

I guess... sometimes I do get a little down on myself. Just, you know, about how things turned out. Losing my old place and... well, just not keeping my life together the way I should have. I feel bad about that, like I let people down. 

And, um, I know this probably sounds silly, but sometimes I worry about things at work or the church pantry. Like if the pantry is short on cans one week, or if a hallway at the building doesn't look perfect, I get this feeling like... like maybe it's my fault? Even if I didn't do it. I just feel like I should be doing more to fix everything. 

But I try not to let it show! I don't want to be a burden on anyone. Is it normal to worry like that sometimes? I really am trying my best to just focus on doing a good job.

Um... harming myself? Oh, no, no, I don't want you to think I'm crazy or that I'm going to do something drastic. I really don't want to be a problem for anyone, I promise. I know how important it is to keep going.

I guess... well, I don't have some big plan or anything like that. I would never want to put that on the people at work or the church. But... um... lately, a lot of mornings, I wake up and I just think... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up at all?

But... well, I guess it just comes back to what I was saying a minute ago. That's really the main way death comes into my mind. Just that thought about myself. And like I said, it scares me enough to actually say it out loud to you, even though I really hate sounding so gloomy. 

I'm sorry if I'm just repeating myself... I don't really think about death outside of that morning feeling. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is that what you were asking? I hope I'm doing this right.

Um... improvement? Oh, yes, actually! I don't want you to think it's all bad all the time. I really do try to get better, I promise. 

There was a stretch... maybe for several weeks? My mood actually did lift some. I was getting through my meals a bit better, and my cleaning shifts at work didn't feel quite so heavy. I was really proud of that, and I was hoping it meant I was finally turning a corner for good.

I'm sorry if that sounds like I'm not trying hard enough. I really am trying to push through it, I promise. Is that... does that make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: Um... sad or empty? Well, I wouldn't want to use a strong word like \"empty.\" That sounds so dramatic, doesn't it? I really don't want to come across like I'm whining or just feeling sorry for myself. \n\nI guess... maybe I feel a little down sometimes. Just kind of flat, you know? Like I'm just going through the motions. But I wouldn't say I'm sitting around crying or anything like that. I still get up and go to work and do my job. I try to keep a smile on my face so the people in the building don't have to deal with my bad moods. \n\nIs it okay to just feel a little flat sometimes? I hope that's a normal way to feel at my age. I really do try to stay positive as much as I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... weight changes? Well, I don't want you to think I'm not taking care of myself or anything. I'm really trying to keep up with things. \n\nI guess... maybe I have lost a little bit of weight. Nothing too crazy, I promise. It's just... lately, most days, food just tastes kind of like cardboard to me. I just don't want to force it, you know? \n\nI did notice my work pants have been hanging a little looser than they used to. But that's probably just from being on my feet all day at the building, right? I hope that's not something you're too worried about. I can try to eat a little more if you think I should, I really don't mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're moving more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... moving more slowly? Well, I don't want you to think I'm slacking off at work or anything. I always make sure my section of the building is completely done before I clock out, I really do. \n\nI guess... maybe I take a little longer to get going in the mornings? Just a little bit. It's not like I'm dragging my feet on purpose, I promise. It just feels like... like my limbs are a little heavier than they used to be. But I'm 59, right? So it's probably just normal getting older stuff, don't you think? \n\nI still get everything done, though. I just pace myself a little more so I don't get in anyone's way. I hope that's alright? I'm really trying to keep up my usual speed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Um... guilty? Oh, I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, really I do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around complaining.\n\nI guess... sometimes I do get a little down on myself. Just, you know, about how things turned out. Losing my old place and... well, just not keeping my life together the way I should have. I feel bad about that, like I let people down. \n\nAnd, um, I know this probably sounds silly, but sometimes I worry about things at work or the church pantry. Like if the pantry is short on cans one week, or if a hallway at the building doesn't look perfect, I get this feeling like... like maybe it's my fault? Even if I didn't do it. I just feel like I should be doing more to fix everything. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to be a burden on anyone. Is it normal to worry like that sometimes? I know I should probably try harder to enjoy things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or anxious about things?\n\nPatient: Um... anxious? Well, I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person, exactly. I hope I don't give off that impression! \n\nI mean, I have normal worries, I guess. Like making sure I do a good job at work, or hoping I don't get in anyone's way. But I don't think I sit around worrying all the time or anything like that. I try really hard to just take things as they come and not be a burden to anyone. \n\nIs it... is it normal to just have regular, everyday worries? I don't think I have any big anxieties that you'd need to be concerned about, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Um... I know we just touched on this a little bit, and I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much or taking up too much of your time. \n\nBut, yeah, I guess I have lost interest. Like I was saying, I used to really love my Saturday fishing trips, and just having my coffee with the crossword puzzle in the mornings. I even liked work—showing the new guys the ropes, like the best way to strip a floor. It felt good to be helpful, you know? \n\nNow... I don't know, I just let all of that sit. I try to do them sometimes so people don't think I'm being lazy or anything, but they just don't give me anything back anymore. The feeling just isn't there. I'm sorry if that sounds like I'm not trying hard enough. I really am trying to push through it, I promise. Well, I wouldn't want to use a strong word like \"empty.\" That sounds so dramatic, doesn't it? I really don't want to come across like I'm whining or just feeling sorry for myself. \n\nI guess... maybe I feel a little down sometimes. Just kind of flat, you know? Like I'm just going through the motions. But I wouldn't say I'm sitting around crying or anything like that. I still get up and go to work and do my job. I try to keep a smile on my face so the people in the building don't have to deal with my bad moods. \n\nIs it okay to just feel a little flat sometimes? I hope that's a normal way to feel at my age. I really do try to stay positive as much as I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... weight changes? Well, I don't want you to think I'm not taking care of myself or anything. I'm really trying to keep up with things. \n\nI guess... maybe I have lost a little bit of weight. So I might skip a meal here and there, or just have a few crackers after my shift when I get home. I just don't want to force it, you know? \n\nI did notice my work pants have been hanging a little looser than they used to. But that's probably just from being on my feet all day at the building, right? I hope that's not something you're too worried about. I feel bad about that, like I let people down. \n\nAnd, um, I know this probably sounds silly, but sometimes I worry about things at work or the church pantry. Like if the pantry is short on cans one week, or if a hallway at the building doesn't look perfect, I get this feeling like... like maybe it's my fault? Even if I didn't do it. I just feel like I should be doing more to fix everything. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to be a burden on anyone. Is it normal to worry like that sometimes? I really am trying my best to just focus on doing a good job.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... harming myself? Oh, no, no, I don't want you to think I'm crazy or that I'm going to do something drastic. I really don't want to be a problem for anyone, I promise. I know how important it is to keep going.\n\nI guess... well, I don't have some big plan or anything like that. I would never want to put that on the people at work or the church. But... um... lately, a lot of mornings, I wake up and I just think... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up at all? \n\nIt's just a passing thought, really! I try to push it right out of my head. But it does happen kind of often, and... well, it actually scares me a little bit to even say it out loud to you. I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: When you have those thoughts about maybe it would be easier if you didn't wake up, do you ever think about how you would make that happen?\n\nPatient: Um... how I would make it happen? And at work, I used to kind of enjoy showing the younger guys some of the tricks I've learned over the years, like the best way to strip a floor. But lately... I don't know, I just mostly let those things sit. It's like they just don't give me anything back anymore, if that makes sense? \n\nI still try to do them sometimes, just so people don't worry about me. Is it okay that I feel that way? I know I should probably try harder to enjoy things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or anxious about things?\n\nPatient: Um... anxious? Well, I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person, exactly. I hope I don't give off that impression! \n\nI mean, I have normal worries, I guess. Like making sure I do a good job at work, or hoping I don't get in anyone's way. But I don't think I sit around worrying all the time or anything like that. I try really hard to just take things as they come and not be a burden to anyone. \n\nIs it... is it normal to just have regular, everyday worries? I don't think I have any big anxieties that you'd need to be concerned about, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Um... I know we just touched on this a little bit, and I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much or taking up too much of your time. \n\nBut, yeah, I guess I have lost interest. Like I was saying, I used to really love my Saturday fishing trips, and just having my coffee with the crossword puzzle in the mornings. I even liked work—showing the new guys the ropes, like the best way to strip a floor. It felt good to be helpful, you know? \n\nNow... I don't know, I just let all of that sit. I try to do them sometimes so people don't think I'm being lazy or anything, but they just don't give me anything back anymore. The feeling just isn't there. I'm sorry if that sounds like I'm not trying hard enough. I really am trying to push through it, I promise. Is that... does that make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: Um... sad or empty? Well, I wouldn't want to use a strong word like \"empty.\" That sounds so dramatic, doesn't it? I really don't want to come across like I'm whining or just feeling sorry for myself. \n\nI guess... maybe I feel a little down sometimes. Just kind of flat, you know? Like I'm just going through the motions. But I wouldn't say I'm sitting around crying or anything like that. I still get up and go to work and do my job. I try to keep a smile on my face so the people in the building don't have to deal with my bad moods. \n\nIs it okay to just feel a little flat sometimes? I hope that's a normal way to feel at my age. I really do try to stay positive as much as I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... weight changes? Well, I don't want you to think I'm not taking care of myself or anything. I'm really trying to keep up with things. \n\nI guess... maybe I have lost a little bit of weight. Nothing too crazy, I promise. It's just... lately, most days, food just tastes kind of like cardboard to me. So I might skip a meal here and there, or just have a few crackers after my shift when I get home. I just don't want to force it, you know? \n\nI did notice my work pants have been hanging a little looser than they used to. But that's probably just from being on my feet all day at the building, right? I hope that's not something you're too worried about. I can try to eat a little more if you think I should, I really don't mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're moving more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... moving more slowly? Well, I don't want you to think I'm slacking off at work or anything. I always make sure my section of the building is completely done before I clock out, I really do. \n\nI guess... maybe I take a little longer to get going in the mornings? Just a little bit. It's not like I'm dragging my feet on purpose, I promise. It just feels like... like my limbs are a little heavier than they used to be. But I'm 59, right? So it's probably just normal getting older stuff, don't you think? \n\nI still get everything done, though. I just pace myself a little more so I don't get in anyone's way. I hope that's alright? I'm really trying to keep up my usual speed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Um... guilty? Oh, I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, really I do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around complaining.\n\nI guess... sometimes I do get a little down on myself. Just, you know, about how things turned out. Losing my old place and... well, just not keeping my life together the way I should have. I feel bad about that, like I let people down. \n\nAnd, um, I know this probably sounds silly, but sometimes I worry about things at work or the church pantry. Like if the pantry is short on cans one week, or if a hallway at the building doesn't look perfect, I get this feeling like... like maybe it's my fault? Even if I didn't do it. I just feel like I should be doing more to fix everything. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to be a burden on anyone. Is it normal to worry like that sometimes? I really am trying my best to just focus on doing a good job.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... harming myself? Oh, no, no, I don't want you to think I'm crazy or that I'm going to do something drastic. I really don't want to be a problem for anyone, I promise. I know how important it is to keep going.\n\nI guess... well, I don't have some big plan or anything like that. I would never want to put that on the people at work or the church. I try to push it right out of my head. But it does happen kind of often, and... well, it actually scares me a little bit to even say it out loud to you. I hope you're not upset with me for feeling that way. I'm really trying to stay positive, I am. Is it okay that I told you that? Um... well, it's not just when I'm feeling down. I... I know I mentioned that they were still there even when my mood lifted for those few weeks. But... um... they definitely happen when I'm feeling really bad, too. 

I guess I should tell you... when I was at my absolute lowest point—when I was barely eating anything, and I felt so guilty all the time, and... and I was even having those thoughts about not wanting to wake up anymore... I was still hearing that man's voice calling me in the stairwell at work. And I was still so sure that the people in the building were watching me through those cameras. 

I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm just complaining about everything. I know it's a lot, and I feel awful dumping all this on you. I just want to make sure you know that the voice and the cameras... they seem to be there no matter how my mood is doing. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you.

I just don't want to force it, you know? \n\nI did notice my work pants have been hanging a little looser than they used to. But that's probably just from being on my feet all day at the building, right? I hope that's not something you're too worried about. I can try to eat a little more if you think I should, I really don't mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're moving more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... moving more slowly? Well, I don't want you to think I'm slacking off at work or anything. I always make sure my section of the building is completely done before I clock out, I really do. \n\nI guess... maybe I take a little longer to get going in the mornings? Just a little bit. It's not like I'm dragging my feet on purpose, I promise. It just feels like... like my limbs are a little heavier than they used to be. But I'm 59, right? So it's probably just normal getting older stuff, don't you think? \n\nI still get everything done, though. I just pace myself a little more so I don't get in anyone's way. I hope that's alright? I'm really trying to keep up my usual speed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Um... guilty? Oh, I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, really I do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around complaining.\n\nI guess... sometimes I do get a little down on myself. Just, you know, about how things turned out. Losing my old place and... well, just not keeping my life together the way I should have. I feel bad about that, like I let people down. \n\nAnd, um, I know this probably sounds silly, but sometimes I worry about things at work or the church pantry. Like if the pantry is short on cans one week, or if a hallway at the building doesn't look perfect, I get this feeling like... like maybe it's my fault? Even if I didn't do it. I just feel like I should be doing more to fix everything. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to be a burden on anyone. Is it normal to worry like that sometimes? I really am trying my best to just focus on doing a good job.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... harming myself? Oh, no, no, I don't want you to think I'm crazy or that I'm going to do something drastic. I really don't want to be a problem for anyone, I promise. I know how important it is to keep going.\n\nI guess... well, I don't have some big plan or anything like that. I would never want to put that on the people at work or the church. But... um... lately, a lot of mornings, I wake up and I just think... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up at all? \n\nIt's just a passing thought, really! I try to push it right out of my head. But it does happen kind of often, and... well, it actually scares me a little bit to even say it out loud to you. I hope you're not upset with me for feeling that way. I'm really trying to stay positive, I am. Is it okay that I told you that? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: When you have those thoughts about maybe it would be easier if you didn't wake up, do you ever think about how you would make that happen?\n\nPatient: Um... how I would make it happen? Oh, gosh, no. Please don't think I'm sitting around plotting anything like that. I really, really don't have any kind of plan, I promise you. I would never want to cause a mess for anyone to clean up, or... or put that kind of burden on the people at the church. \n\nIt's just... like I was saying, it's just this heavy feeling when I wake up. Just this thought of... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't. But I don't think about *how* to do it. I just... I just have the thought, and it actually scares me that it even pops into my head. That's why I wanted to be honest and tell you, even though I was afraid you might think worse of me for it. \n\nI really am trying to just keep my head down and do my job and be good. I hope it's okay that I don't have a specific answer for the \"how\" part... I just wanted to make sure I was answering you properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death in general, not just in relation to yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... death in general? I try really hard not to be morbid, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around dwelling on dark things or obsessing over it. I really try to keep my mind on my work and staying useful. \n\nBut... well, I guess it just comes back to what I was saying a minute ago. I don't have some big plan or anything like that, but... a lot of mornings I just wake up thinking it might be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. That's really the main way death comes into my mind. Just that thought about myself. And like I said, it scares me enough to actually say it out loud to you, even though I really hate sounding so gloomy. \n\nI'm sorry if I'm just repeating myself... I don't really think about death outside of that morning feeling. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is that what you were asking? I hope I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of significant improvement in your mood over the past year and five months?\n\nPatient: Um... improvement? Oh, yes, actually! I don't want you to think it's all bad all the time. I really do try to get better, I promise. \n\nThere was a stretch... maybe for several weeks? My mood actually did lift some. I was getting through my meals a bit better, and my cleaning shifts at work didn't feel quite so heavy. I was really proud of that, and I was hoping it meant I was finally turning a corner for good.\n\nBut... um... well, I'm a little embarrassed to admit this next part. I really hope you won't be disappointed in me, or... or think I'm completely crazy. Even when my mood was better, I... I still kept hearing someone calling my name when I was working out in the stairwell. And... I stayed pretty convinced that the security cameras in the hallways were being used to track me. \n\nI tried so hard to just keep my head down and ignore it, I really did! I know how strange it sounds, and I didn't want to make a fuss about it at work. I just... I want to make sure I'm giving you the whole picture so you can help me. I hope it's alright that I shared that with you... I'm doing okay answering your questions, right?\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any unusual thoughts or perceptions, like hearing voices or feeling like you're being watched, during those several weeks when your mood was better?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, actually. I... I think I just mentioned that, but I want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking. I hope I'm not being confusing. \n\nDuring those several weeks when my mood lifted—when I was eating better and getting through my cleaning shifts easier—I... well, yes, I still had those other things happening. I was still hearing someone call my name when I was out in the stairwell. And... and I still felt certain that the hallway cameras were tracking me. \n\nI'm really sorry to have to say that again, I know it doesn't sound good. I was so hoping that when my mood got better, all of that would just fade away, too. I really tried to ignore it all and just be grateful I was feeling less heavy. I hope you don't think I wasn't trying hard enough to get completely well... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the exact information you need. Is that what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear or the feeling of being watched still present now?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, they are. I'm sorry to say they are. I really wish I could tell you they stopped, I know that would be the better answer. \n\nIf I think about it... well, the name-calling in the stairwell and the... the worries about the cameras... it's been going on for about 11 months now, all together. I know that sounds like a long time to let something like that go on without saying anything, and I feel terrible about it. I just kept hoping if I worked hard and stayed polite, it would all just sort of resolve itself, you know? \n\nI really hope you aren't upset with me for letting it go on for 11 months. I'm trying to be completely honest with you now, so we can fix it. Does that... does that timeline help you understand what's happening to me?\n\nDoctor: Are the voices or the feeling of being watched only present when you're feeling down, or do they happen at other times as well?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not just when I'm feeling down. I... I know I mentioned that they were still there even when my mood lifted for those few weeks. But... um... they definitely happen when I'm feeling really bad, too. \n\nI guess I should tell you... when I was at my absolute lowest point—when I was barely eating anything, and I felt so guilty all the time, and... and I was even having those thoughts about not wanting to wake up anymore... I was still hearing that man's voice calling me in the stairwell at work. And I was still so sure that the people in the building were watching me through those cameras. \n\nI really hope it doesn't sound like I'm just complaining about everything. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um... well, to be completely honest with you... no, they don't really go away. I know I mentioned this a little bit before, and I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking.

I'm a building cleaner, and usually I'm fine with the work, but recently, even just getting started on my cleaning route feels like pushing a loaded cart uphill. \n\nI mean, I'm still getting my job done, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just being lazy or anything! But... does that make sense? Is it normal to just feel that blank and heavy?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess it's been building up for a little while now. I hope I didn't wait too long to come see you, I really tried to just handle it on my own so I wouldn't be a bother. But I'd say it's been about... maybe a year and five months? \n\nIt sort of started right around the time I had to leave my old apartment and, um, move out of my old neighborhood. I know people move all the time, so I probably shouldn't let it affect me this much, right? I tried to just keep my head down and not make a fuss about it. But yeah, ever since then, it's just been slowly getting heavier. I hope that gives you the right timeline?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. I try to keep busy, I really do. \n\nBut... well, I used to really look forward to going fishing on Saturdays, or just sitting down with a cup of coffee and doing the crossword puzzle. And at work, I used to kind of enjoy showing the younger guys some of the tricks I've learned over the years, like the best way to strip a floor. But lately... I don't know, I just mostly let those things sit. It's like they just don't give me anything back anymore, if that makes sense? \n\nI still try to do them sometimes, just so people don't worry about me. Is it okay that I feel that way? I know I should probably try harder to enjoy things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or anxious about things?\n\nPatient: Um... anxious? Well, I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person, exactly. I hope I don't give off that impression! \n\nI mean, I have normal worries, I guess. Like making sure I do a good job at work, or hoping I don't get in anyone's way. But I don't think I sit around worrying all the time or anything like that. I try really hard to just take things as they come and not be a burden to anyone. \n\nIs it... is it normal to just have regular, everyday worries? I don't think I have any big anxieties that you'd need to be concerned about, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Um... I know we just touched on this a little bit, and I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much or taking up too much of your time. \n\nBut, yeah, I guess I have lost interest. Like I was saying, I used to really love my Saturday fishing trips, and just having my coffee with the crossword puzzle in the mornings. I even liked work—showing the new guys the ropes, like the best way to strip a floor. It felt good to be helpful, you know? \n\nNow... I don't know, I just let all of that sit. I try to do them sometimes so people don't think I'm being lazy or anything, but they just don't give me anything back anymore. The feeling just isn't there. I'm sorry if that sounds like I'm not trying hard enough. I really am trying to push through it, I promise. Is that... does that make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: Um... sad or empty? Well, I wouldn't want to use a strong word like \"empty.\" That sounds so dramatic, doesn't it? I really don't want to come across like I'm whining or just feeling sorry for myself. \n\nI guess... maybe I feel a little down sometimes. Just kind of flat, you know? Like I'm just going through the motions. But I wouldn't say I'm sitting around crying or anything like that. I still get up and go to work and do my job. I try to keep a smile on my face so the people in the building don't have to deal with my bad moods. \n\nIs it okay to just feel a little flat sometimes? I hope that's a normal way to feel at my age. I really do try to stay positive as much as I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... weight changes? Well, I don't want you to think I'm not taking care of myself or anything. I'm really trying to keep up with things. \n\nI guess... maybe I have lost a little bit of weight. Nothing too crazy, I promise. It's just... lately, most days, food just tastes kind of like cardboard to me. So I might skip a meal here and there, or just have a few crackers after my shift when I get home. I just don't want to force it, you know? \n\nI did notice my work pants have been hanging a little looser than they used to. But that's probably just from being on my feet all day at the building, right? I hope that's not something you're too worried about. I can try to eat a little more if you think I should, I really don't mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're moving more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... moving more slowly? Well, I don't want you to think I'm slacking off at work or anything. I always make sure my section of the building is completely done before I clock out, I really do. \n\nI guess... maybe I take a little longer to get going in the mornings? Just a little bit. It's not like I'm dragging my feet on purpose, I promise. It just feels like... like my limbs are a little heavier than they used to be. But I'm 59, right? So it's probably just normal getting older stuff, don't you think? \n\nI still get everything done, though. I just pace myself a little more so I don't get in anyone's way. I hope that's alright? I'm really trying to keep up my usual speed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Um... guilty? Oh, I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, really I do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around complaining.\n\nI guess... sometimes I do get a little down on myself. Just, you know, about how things turned out. Losing my old place and... well, just not keeping my life together the way I should have. I feel bad about that, like I let people down. \n\nAnd, um, I know this probably sounds silly, but sometimes I worry about things at work or the church pantry. Like if the pantry is short on cans one week, or if a hallway at the building doesn't look perfect, I get this feeling like... like maybe it's my fault? Even if I didn't do it. I just feel like I should be doing more to fix everything. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to be a burden on anyone. Is it normal to worry like that sometimes? I really am trying my best to just focus on doing a good job.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... harming myself? Oh, no, no, I don't want you to think I'm crazy or that I'm going to do something drastic. I really don't want to be a problem for anyone, I promise. I know how important it is to keep going.\n\nI guess... well, I don't have some big plan or anything like that. I would never want to put that on the people at work or the church. But... um... lately, a lot of mornings, I wake up and I just think... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up at all? \n\nIt's just a passing thought, really! I try to push it right out of my head. But it does happen kind of often, and... well, it actually scares me a little bit to even say it out loud to you. I hope you're not upset with me for feeling that way. I'm really trying to stay positive, I am. Is it okay that I told you that? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: When you have those thoughts about maybe it would be easier if you didn't wake up, do you ever think about how you would make that happen?\n\nPatient: Um... how I would make it happen? Oh, gosh, no. Please don't think I'm sitting around plotting anything like that. I really, really don't have any kind of plan, I promise you. I would never want to cause a mess for anyone to clean up, or... or put that kind of burden on the people at the church. \n\nIt's just... like I was saying, it's just this heavy feeling when I wake up. Just this thought of... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't. But I don't think about *how* to do it. I just... I just have the thought, and it actually scares me that it even pops into my head. That's why I wanted to be honest and tell you, even though I was afraid you might think worse of me for it. \n\nI really am trying to just keep my head down and do my job and be good. I hope it's okay that I don't have a specific answer for the \"how\" part... I just wanted to make sure I was answering you properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death in general, not just in relation to yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... death in general? I try really hard not to be morbid, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around dwelling on dark things or obsessing over it. I really try to keep my mind on my work and staying useful. \n\nBut... well, I guess it just comes back to what I was saying a minute ago. I don't have some big plan or anything like that, but... a lot of mornings I just wake up thinking it might be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. Um... yes, they are. I... I know I just mentioned this, and I really hope I'm not being annoying by repeating myself. I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right.

I just don't want to force it, you know? \n\nI did notice my work pants have been hanging a little looser than they used to. But that's probably just from being on my feet all day at the building, right? I hope that's not something you're too worried about. I can try to eat a little more if you think I should, I really don't mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're moving more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... moving more slowly? Well, I don't want you to think I'm slacking off at work or anything. I always make sure my section of the building is completely done before I clock out, I really do. \n\nI guess... maybe I take a little longer to get going in the mornings? Just a little bit. It's not like I'm dragging my feet on purpose, I promise. It just feels like... like my limbs are a little heavier than they used to be. But I'm 59, right? So it's probably just normal getting older stuff, don't you think? \n\nI still get everything done, though. I just pace myself a little more so I don't get in anyone's way. I hope that's alright? I'm really trying to keep up my usual speed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Um... guilty? Oh, I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, really I do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around complaining.\n\nI guess... sometimes I do get a little down on myself. Just, you know, about how things turned out. Losing my old place and... well, just not keeping my life together the way I should have. I feel bad about that, like I let people down. \n\nAnd, um, I know this probably sounds silly, but sometimes I worry about things at work or the church pantry. Like if the pantry is short on cans one week, or if a hallway at the building doesn't look perfect, I get this feeling like... like maybe it's my fault? Even if I didn't do it. I just feel like I should be doing more to fix everything. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to be a burden on anyone. Is it normal to worry like that sometimes? I really am trying my best to just focus on doing a good job.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... harming myself? Oh, no, no, I don't want you to think I'm crazy or that I'm going to do something drastic. I really don't want to be a problem for anyone, I promise. I know how important it is to keep going.\n\nI guess... well, I don't have some big plan or anything like that. I would never want to put that on the people at work or the church. But... um... lately, a lot of mornings, I wake up and I just think... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up at all? \n\nIt's just a passing thought, really! I try to push it right out of my head. But it does happen kind of often, and... well, it actually scares me a little bit to even say it out loud to you. I hope you're not upset with me for feeling that way. I'm really trying to stay positive, I am. Is it okay that I told you that? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: When you have those thoughts about maybe it would be easier if you didn't wake up, do you ever think about how you would make that happen?\n\nPatient: Um... how I would make it happen? Oh, gosh, no. Please don't think I'm sitting around plotting anything like that. I really, really don't have any kind of plan, I promise you. I would never want to cause a mess for anyone to clean up, or... or put that kind of burden on the people at the church. \n\nIt's just... like I was saying, it's just this heavy feeling when I wake up. Just this thought of... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't. But I don't think about *how* to do it. I just... I just have the thought, and it actually scares me that it even pops into my head. That's why I wanted to be honest and tell you, even though I was afraid you might think worse of me for it. \n\nI really am trying to just keep my head down and do my job and be good. I hope it's okay that I don't have a specific answer for the \"how\" part... I just wanted to make sure I was answering you properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death in general, not just in relation to yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... death in general? I try really hard not to be morbid, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around dwelling on dark things or obsessing over it. I really try to keep my mind on my work and staying useful. \n\nBut... well, I guess it just comes back to what I was saying a minute ago. I don't have some big plan or anything like that, but... a lot of mornings I just wake up thinking it might be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. That's really the main way death comes into my mind. Just that thought about myself. And like I said, it scares me enough to actually say it out loud to you, even though I really hate sounding so gloomy. \n\nI'm sorry if I'm just repeating myself... I don't really think about death outside of that morning feeling. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is that what you were asking? I hope I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of significant improvement in your mood over the past year and five months?\n\nPatient: Um... improvement? Oh, yes, actually! I don't want you to think it's all bad all the time. I really do try to get better, I promise. \n\nThere was a stretch... maybe for several weeks? My mood actually did lift some. I was getting through my meals a bit better, and my cleaning shifts at work didn't feel quite so heavy. I was really proud of that, and I was hoping it meant I was finally turning a corner for good.\n\nBut... um... well, I'm a little embarrassed to admit this next part. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, to be perfectly honest... no. I know we just talked about this, and I'm really sorry if I'm making you ask the same things over again. I promise I'm trying to give you good answers. 

But no, they don't really stop. Like I was saying, there was that time... a few weeks, maybe... where my mood was actually pretty good. I was eating better, getting through my shifts without feeling so heavy... I was really proud of how well I was doing at work, actually. I know that's probably what's supposed to happen, and I don't want you to think I'm not trying my hardest to get well. I really am. I hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

I really do try to stay positive as much as I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... weight changes? Well, I don't want you to think I'm not taking care of myself or anything. I'm really trying to keep up with things. \n\nI guess... maybe I have lost a little bit of weight. Nothing too crazy, I promise. It's just... lately, most days, food just tastes kind of like cardboard to me. So I might skip a meal here and there, or just have a few crackers after my shift when I get home. I just don't want to force it, you know? \n\nI did notice my work pants have been hanging a little looser than they used to. But that's probably just from being on my feet all day at the building, right? I hope that's not something you're too worried about. I can try to eat a little more if you think I should, I really don't mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're moving more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... moving more slowly? Well, I don't want you to think I'm slacking off at work or anything. I always make sure my section of the building is completely done before I clock out, I really do. \n\nI guess... maybe I take a little longer to get going in the mornings? Just a little bit. It's not like I'm dragging my feet on purpose, I promise. It just feels like... like my limbs are a little heavier than they used to be. But I'm 59, right? So it's probably just normal getting older stuff, don't you think? \n\nI still get everything done, though. I just pace myself a little more so I don't get in anyone's way. I hope that's alright? I'm really trying to keep up my usual speed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Um... guilty? Oh, I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, really I do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around complaining.\n\nI guess... sometimes I do get a little down on myself. Just, you know, about how things turned out. Losing my old place and... well, just not keeping my life together the way I should have. I feel bad about that, like I let people down. \n\nAnd, um, I know this probably sounds silly, but sometimes I worry about things at work or the church pantry. Like if the pantry is short on cans one week, or if a hallway at the building doesn't look perfect, I get this feeling like... like maybe it's my fault? Even if I didn't do it. I just feel like I should be doing more to fix everything. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to be a burden on anyone. Is it normal to worry like that sometimes? I really am trying my best to just focus on doing a good job.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... harming myself? Oh, no, no, I don't want you to think I'm crazy or that I'm going to do something drastic. I really don't want to be a problem for anyone, I promise. I know how important it is to keep going.\n\nI guess... well, I don't have some big plan or anything like that. I would never want to put that on the people at work or the church. But... um... lately, a lot of mornings, I wake up and I just think... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up at all? \n\nIt's just a passing thought, really! I try to push it right out of my head. But it does happen kind of often, and... well, it actually scares me a little bit to even say it out loud to you. I hope you're not upset with me for feeling that way. I'm really trying to stay positive, I am. Is it okay that I told you that? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: When you have those thoughts about maybe it would be easier if you didn't wake up, do you ever think about how you would make that happen?\n\nPatient: Um... how I would make it happen? Oh, gosh, no. Please don't think I'm sitting around plotting anything like that. I really, really don't have any kind of plan, I promise you. I would never want to cause a mess for anyone to clean up, or... or put that kind of burden on the people at the church. \n\nIt's just... like I was saying, it's just this heavy feeling when I wake up. Just this thought of... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't. But I don't think about *how* to do it. I just... I just have the thought, and it actually scares me that it even pops into my head. That's why I wanted to be honest and tell you, even though I was afraid you might think worse of me for it. \n\nI really am trying to just keep my head down and do my job and be good. I hope it's okay that I don't have a specific answer for the \"how\" part... I just wanted to make sure I was answering you properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death in general, not just in relation to yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... death in general? I try really hard not to be morbid, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around dwelling on dark things or obsessing over it. I really try to keep my mind on my work and staying useful. \n\nBut... well, I guess it just comes back to what I was saying a minute ago. I don't have some big plan or anything like that, but... a lot of mornings I just wake up thinking it might be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. That's really the main way death comes into my mind. Just that thought about myself. And like I said, it scares me enough to actually say it out loud to you, even though I really hate sounding so gloomy. \n\nI'm sorry if I'm just repeating myself... I don't really think about death outside of that morning feeling. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is that what you were asking? I hope I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of significant improvement in your mood over the past year and five months?\n\nPatient: Um... improvement? Oh, yes, actually! I don't want you to think it's all bad all the time. I really do try to get better, I promise. \n\nThere was a stretch... maybe for several weeks? My mood actually did lift some. I was getting through my meals a bit better, and my cleaning shifts at work didn't feel quite so heavy. I was really proud of that, and I was hoping it meant I was finally turning a corner for good.\n\nBut... um... well, I'm a little embarrassed to admit this next part. I hope it's alright that I shared that with you... I was still hearing that man's voice calling me in the stairwell at work. My mood had lifted some, and I wasn't struggling quite so much. I was getting through my meals, and work felt a little easier to manage, which was really nice. I didn't want to cause any problems or seem paranoid. But it just stayed there, even when I was feeling my best. I hope it's okay that I'm telling you this...
